%% Space Visual Dictionary
\documentclass[12pt,a4paper,twoside]{book}

\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}
\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}
\usepackage{lmodern}
\usepackage{microtype}

\usepackage[top=2.5cm, bottom=2.5cm, left=2.5cm, right=2.5cm]{geometry}
\usepackage{multicol}
\setlength{\columnsep}{1.2em}
\setlength{\multicolsep}{0pt}

\usepackage{fancyhdr}
\pagestyle{fancy}
\fancyhf{}
\fancyhead[LE,RO]{\leftmark}
\fancyhead[LO,RE]{\thepage}
\setlength{\headheight}{14.5pt}
\renewcommand{\headrulewidth}{0.4pt}

\usepackage{xcolor}
\definecolor{catAD}{RGB}{0,90,150}
\definecolor{catAE}{RGB}{50,120,200}
\definecolor{catRM}{RGB}{180,30,30}
\definecolor{catD}{RGB}{20,140,130}
\definecolor{catSS}{RGB}{120,40,160}

\usepackage[style=altlist,nonumberlist,nopostdot]{glossaries}
\makeglossaries

\usepackage[hidelinks]{hyperref}

\newcommand{\dictentry}[3]{%
  \par\addvspace{3pt}%
  \hangindent=0pt
  \hangafter=0
  \noindent{\color{#3}\textbf{#1}}\hspace{0.5em}{#2}\par
}

\newcommand{\booktitle}{Space Visual Dictionary}
\title{\Huge \booktitle}
\author{}
\date{}

\clearpage
\chapter*{Aerodynamics}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Aerodynamics}
\markboth{Aerodynamics}{Aerodynamics}
\clearpage
\begin{multicols}{2}
\small
The study of air in motion and the forces it creates on solid bodies. From boundary layers to shock waves, airfoils to wind tunnels.\par
\vspace{0.5cm}
\glsadd{AD-aerodynamics}\dictentry{Aerodynamics}{The study of how air flows around solid objects and the forces generated by moving air.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aerodynamic-center}\dictentry{Aerodynamic Center}{The point on an airfoil about which the pitching moment is constant regardless of angle of attack.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aerodynamic-force}\dictentry{Aerodynamic Force}{The resultant force on a body moving through air, resolved into lift, drag, and side force.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aerodynamic-heating}\dictentry{Aerodynamic Heating}{Frictional heating caused by the compression of air at high speeds.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aeroelasticity}\dictentry{Aeroelasticity}{The study of interaction between aerodynamic forces and structural deformation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aileron}\dictentry{Aileron}{A hinged control surface on the trailing edge of a wing that controls roll.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-airfoil}\dictentry{Airfoil}{A cross-sectional shape designed to produce lift or control from the air.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-angle-of-attack}\dictentry{Angle of Attack}{The angle between the chord line of an airfoil and the oncoming airflow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-angle-of-incidence}\dictentry{Angle of Incidence}{The angle between the wing chord line and the aircraft longitudinal axis.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-anhedral}\dictentry{Anhedral}{A downward angle of the wings decreasing lateral stability.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aspect-ratio}\dictentry{Aspect Ratio}{The ratio of wingspan to mean chord. Higher AR gives lower induced drag.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-attached-flow}\dictentry{Attached Flow}{Airflow that smoothly follows the contour of a surface.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-autorotation}\dictentry{Autorotation}{The condition where a rotor turns due to airflow passing through it.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-boundary-layer}\dictentry{Boundary Layer}{The thin viscous region of flow adjacent to a surface.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-boundary-layer-separation}\dictentry{Boundary Layer Separation}{Detachment of the boundary layer from the surface, causing a wake.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-boundary-layer-transition}\dictentry{Boundary Layer Transition}{The change from laminar to turbulent flow in the boundary layer.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-camber}\dictentry{Camber}{The curvature of the mean camber line of an airfoil.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-center-of-pressure}\dictentry{Center of Pressure}{The point on an airfoil where the resultant aerodynamic force acts.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-chord}\dictentry{Chord}{The straight-line distance from the leading edge to the trailing edge of an airfoil.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-chord-line}\dictentry{Chord Line}{The imaginary line connecting the leading and trailing edges.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-circulation}\dictentry{Circulation}{The line integral of velocity around a closed curve, proportional to lift.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-compressible-flow}\dictentry{Compressible Flow}{Flow in which density variations are significant.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-critical-angle-of-attack}\dictentry{Critical Angle of Attack}{The angle of attack at which the wing stalls.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-critical-mach-number}\dictentry{Critical Mach Number}{The freestream Mach at which sonic flow first appears on the airframe.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-dihedral}\dictentry{Dihedral}{The upward angle of wings increasing lateral stability.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AE-downwash}\dictentry{Downwash}{The downward deflection of air behind a wing, contributing to induced drag.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-drag}\dictentry{Drag}{The aerodynamic force opposing motion through the air.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-drag-coefficient}\dictentry{Drag Coefficient}{A dimensionless coefficient relating drag to dynamic pressure and area.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-dynamic-stall}\dictentry{Dynamic Stall}{A delayed stall from rapid change in angle of attack, with temporary vortex lift.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-dynamic-stability}\dictentry{Dynamic Stability}{The evolution of aircraft motion over time after a disturbance.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-dutch-roll}\dictentry{Dutch Roll}{A coupled lateral-directional oscillation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-elevator}\dictentry{Elevator}{A horizontal control surface on the tail that controls pitch.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-elevon}\dictentry{Elevon}{A combined elevator and aileron control surface on tailless aircraft.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-expansion-wave}\dictentry{Expansion Wave}{A wave that expands and accelerates supersonic flow around a convex corner.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-flap}\dictentry{Flap}{A high-lift device on the trailing edge that increases camber and lift.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AE-flow-separation}\dictentry{Flow Separation}{Detachment of boundary layer from a surface.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-form-drag}\dictentry{Form Drag}{Drag due to the shape of the body.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-friction-drag}\dictentry{Friction Drag}{Drag due to shear stress between the surface and the fluid.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-hypersonic-flow}\dictentry{Hypersonic Flow}{Flow with Mach number greater than 5.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-incompressible-flow}\dictentry{Incompressible Flow}{Flow in which density is assumed constant.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-induced-drag}\dictentry{Induced Drag}{Drag created as a consequence of producing lift.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-induced-velocity}\dictentry{Induced Velocity}{The downward velocity component imparted to the air by a lifting wing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-interference-drag}\dictentry{Interference Drag}{Drag from the interaction of flow fields between different components.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-inviscid-flow}\dictentry{Inviscid Flow}{An idealized flow neglecting viscosity effects.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-irrotational-flow}\dictentry{Irrotational Flow}{Flow in which fluid particles have zero net rotation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-kutta-condition}\dictentry{Kutta Condition}{The principle that flow leaves the sharp trailing edge smoothly.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-kutta-joukowski-theorem}\dictentry{Kutta-Joukowski Theorem}{Lift per unit span equals density times velocity times circulation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-laminar-flow}\dictentry{Laminar Flow}{Smooth, orderly fluid motion with distinct parallel layers.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lateral-stability}\dictentry{Lateral Stability}{Stability about the longitudinal axis, including roll.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-leading-edge}\dictentry{Leading Edge}{The forward edge of an airfoil or wing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lift}\dictentry{Lift}{The aerodynamic force perpendicular to the relative wind supporting the aircraft.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lift-coefficient}\dictentry{Lift Coefficient}{A dimensionless coefficient relating lift to dynamic pressure and area.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-longitudinal-stability}\dictentry{Longitudinal Stability}{Stability about the lateral axis concerning pitch motion.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-mach-angle}\dictentry{Mach Angle}{The angle of the Mach cone: mu = arcsin(1/M).}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-mach-cone}\dictentry{Mach Cone}{The cone-shaped wavefront from a supersonic body.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-mean-aerodynamic-chord}\dictentry{Mean Aerodynamic Chord}{The chord of an equivalent rectangular wing with identical moment characteristics.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-momentum-thickness}\dictentry{Momentum Thickness}{A measure of the momentum deficit in the boundary layer.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-navier-stokes-equations}\dictentry{Navier-Stokes Equations}{The fundamental equations describing viscous fluid motion.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-neutral-point}\dictentry{Neutral Point}{The point where pitching moment is independent of angle of attack.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-normal-shock}\dictentry{Normal Shock}{A shock wave perpendicular to the flow direction.}{catAD}
\glsadd{RM-nozzle}\dictentry{Nozzle}{A duct that accelerates a fluid to produce thrust.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-oblique-shock}\dictentry{Oblique Shock}{A shock wave inclined to the direction of supersonic flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-parasite-drag}\dictentry{Parasite Drag}{All drag not associated with lift production.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-phugoid}\dictentry{Phugoid}{A long-period longitudinal oscillation exchanging kinetic and potential energy.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-pitching-moment}\dictentry{Pitching Moment}{The aerodynamic moment about the lateral axis.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-pitot-tube}\dictentry{Pitot Tube}{A tube facing the flow to measure stagnation pressure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-prandtl-meyer-expansion}\dictentry{Prandtl-Meyer Expansion}{Isentropic expansion of supersonic flow around a convex corner.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-pressure-drag}\dictentry{Pressure Drag}{Drag from the pressure difference between front and rear of a body.}{catAD}
\glsadd{D-propeller}\dictentry{Propeller}{A rotating airfoil that converts engine power into thrust.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-reattachment}\dictentry{Reattachment}{The re-establishment of attached flow after separation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-relative-wind}\dictentry{Relative Wind}{The direction of airflow relative to a moving airfoil.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-reynolds-number}\dictentry{Reynolds Number}{The ratio of inertial to viscous forces in a flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-rolling-moment}\dictentry{Rolling Moment}{The aerodynamic moment about the longitudinal axis.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-rotor}\dictentry{Rotor}{A rotating wing assembly that generates lift and propulsion.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-rudder}\dictentry{Rudder}{A vertical control surface on the tail that controls yaw.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-schlieren-photography}\dictentry{Schlieren Photography}{An optical technique for imaging density gradients in flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-separated-flow}\dictentry{Separated Flow}{Flow that has detached from the surface.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-shock-wave}\dictentry{Shock Wave}{A thin region across which flow properties change abruptly.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-short-period-mode}\dictentry{Short Period Mode}{A well-damped, high-frequency longitudinal oscillation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-skin-friction}\dictentry{Skin Friction}{The shear resistance between the surface and the fluid.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-skin-friction-drag}\dictentry{Skin Friction Drag}{Drag due to shear stress on the surface.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-slat}\dictentry{Slat}{A leading-edge high-lift device that extends to increase maximum lift.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-sonic-boom}\dictentry{Sonic Boom}{The audible shock wave from a supersonic aircraft reaching the ground.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-spoiler}\dictentry{Spoiler}{A surface that disrupts lift and increases drag for roll control or speed reduction.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-stall}\dictentry{Stall}{Flow separation from the wing due to excessive angle of attack.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-static-margin}\dictentry{Static Margin}{The distance between the neutral point and CG, determining static stability.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-static-port}\dictentry{Static Port}{An opening used to measure static pressure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-static-stability}\dictentry{Static Stability}{The initial tendency to return to equilibrium after a disturbance.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-steady-flow}\dictentry{Steady Flow}{Flow where properties at a point are constant over time.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-subsonic-flow}\dictentry{Subsonic Flow}{Flow with Mach number less than 1.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-supersonic-flow}\dictentry{Supersonic Flow}{Flow with Mach number greater than 1.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-sweep-angle}\dictentry{Sweep Angle}{The angle between the wing quarter-chord line and the lateral axis.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-thickness-ratio}\dictentry{Thickness Ratio}{The ratio of maximum thickness to chord of an airfoil.}{catAD}
\glsadd{RM-thrust}\dictentry{Thrust}{The propulsive force moving the aircraft forward.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-thrust-vectoring}\dictentry{Thrust Vectoring}{The ability to redirect engine thrust for attitude control.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-trailing-edge}\dictentry{Trailing Edge}{The aft edge of an airfoil or wing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-transonic-flow}\dictentry{Transonic Flow}{Flow in the Mach 0.8-1.2 range with mixed subsonic/supersonic regions.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-trim-drag}\dictentry{Trim Drag}{Drag from deflecting control surfaces to maintain trimmed flight.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AE-turbofan}\dictentry{Turbofan}{A ducted jet engine where a fan provides a large portion of thrust.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AE-turbojet}\dictentry{Turbojet}{A gas turbine engine producing thrust by accelerating a jet rearward.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-turbulent-flow}\dictentry{Turbulent Flow}{Chaotic, irregular fluid motion characterized by eddies.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-unsteady-flow}\dictentry{Unsteady Flow}{Flow where properties at a point vary with time.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-upwash}\dictentry{Upwash}{The upward flow ahead of a wing that influences surfaces in front.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-vortex}\dictentry{Vortex}{A region of rotating fluid, often forming at wingtips.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-vortex-generator}\dictentry{Vortex Generator}{A small vane that energizes the boundary layer to delay separation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wake}\dictentry{Wake}{The region of disturbed flow behind a body.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wall-shear-stress}\dictentry{Wall Shear Stress}{The frictional stress exerted by the flow on the surface.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-washout}\dictentry{Washout}{A wing twist that reduces angle of attack at the tip relative to the root.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wave-drag}\dictentry{Wave Drag}{Drag from shock wave formation in transonic and supersonic flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wing}\dictentry{Wing}{The primary lifting surface of an aircraft.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wing-area}\dictentry{Wing Area}{The planform surface area of the wing used in lift/drag calculations.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wing-span}\dictentry{Wing Span}{The distance from one wingtip to the other.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-winglet}\dictentry{Winglet}{A vertical tip device that reduces induced drag by modifying the tip vortex.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wing-root}\dictentry{Wing Root}{The junction where the wing meets the fuselage.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wing-tip}\dictentry{Wing Tip}{The extreme outer end of the wing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-yawing-moment}\dictentry{Yawing Moment}{The aerodynamic moment about the vertical axis.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-adverse-yaw}\dictentry{Adverse Yaw}{The tendency to yaw opposite to the roll direction due to differential drag.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-airbrake}\dictentry{Airbrake}{A device that increases drag to decelerate an aircraft in flight.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-boundary-layer-control}\dictentry{Boundary Layer Control}{Methods to manipulate the state of the boundary layer.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-choked-flow}\dictentry{Choked Flow}{A duct condition where mass flow is limited by the throat reaching Mach 1.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-computational-fluid-dynamics}\dictentry{Computational Fluid Dynamics}{Numerical methods to solve fluid flow problems.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-contra-rotating-propeller}\dictentry{Contra-Rotating Propeller}{Two coaxial propellers rotating in opposite directions.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-diffuser}\dictentry{Diffuser}{A duct that decelerates flow, increasing its pressure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-displacement-thickness}\dictentry{Displacement Thickness}{The outward displacement of external flow by boundary layer slowing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-divergence}\dictentry{Divergence}{An instability where a disturbance increases in magnitude.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-euler-equations}\dictentry{Euler Equations}{The Navier-Stokes equations without viscosity and thermal terms.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-finite-element-method}\dictentry{Finite Element Method}{A numerical technique for approximate boundary value solutions.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-finite-volume-method}\dictentry{Finite Volume Method}{A numerical method using control volumes to solve PDEs.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-force-balance}\dictentry{Force Balance}{A device measuring aerodynamic forces on a wind tunnel model.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-grid}\dictentry{Grid}{The discretized representation of a fluid domain.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-les}\dictentry{LES}{Large Eddy Simulation, solving for large-scale turbulent eddies.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-mach-number}\dictentry{Mach Number}{The ratio of flow speed to the local speed of sound.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-mesh}\dictentry{Mesh}{The cells or nodes forming a discretized fluid volume.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-particle-image-velocimetry}\dictentry{Particle Image Velocimetry}{An optical flow measurement technique using particle imaging.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-ramjet}\dictentry{Ramjet}{An air-breathing engine using forward motion for compression.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-rans}\dictentry{RANS}{Reynolds-Averaged Navier-Stokes, a time-averaged CFD approach.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-dns}\dictentry{DNS}{Direct Numerical Simulation, solving all turbulent scales directly.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-scramjet}\dictentry{Scramjet}{A supersonic ramjet with supersonic internal flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-smoke-visualization}\dictentry{Smoke Visualization}{Using smoke to visualize flow patterns over a model.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-static-test}\dictentry{Static Test}{Structural testing under predetermined loads.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-test-section}\dictentry{Test Section}{The wind tunnel area where models are placed in uniform flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-transitional-flow}\dictentry{Transitional Flow}{Flow between laminar and turbulent states.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-turbulence-model}\dictentry{Turbulence Model}{A model for Reynolds stresses in turbulent CFD.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-vortex-shedding}\dictentry{Vortex Shedding}{Alternating vortices shed from a bluff body in a flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wind-tunnel}\dictentry{Wind Tunnel}{An experimental facility generating controlled airflow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aerothermal-heating}\dictentry{Aerothermal Heating}{Heating of a body due to high-speed atmospheric passage.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-ablation}\dictentry{Ablation}{Surface material removal by vaporization for thermal protection.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-re-entry}\dictentry{Re-entry}{The return of a spacecraft to the atmosphere.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-shock-layer}\dictentry{Shock Layer}{The hot shocked gas between the bow shock and vehicle surface.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-thermal-protection-system}\dictentry{Thermal Protection System}{Materials protecting a spacecraft from re-entry heating.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-pulsed-detonation-engine}\dictentry{Pulsed Detonation Engine}{An engine using detonation waves for propulsion.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-turbine}\dictentry{Turbine}{A rotary device extracting energy from flowing fluid.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-compressor}\dictentry{Compressor}{A device increasing the pressure of a gas.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-spoileron}\dictentry{Spoileron}{A spoiler used differentially for roll control.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-strake}\dictentry{Strake}{A narrow extension to a wing or fuselage for aerodynamic effect.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-canard}\dictentry{Canard}{A small forewing ahead of the main wing for control or stability.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-blended-wing-body}\dictentry{Blended Wing Body}{An aircraft configuration blending wing and fuselage.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-flying-wing}\dictentry{Flying Wing}{A tailless aircraft where the wing is the primary structure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-delta-wing}\dictentry{Delta Wing}{A triangular planform wing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-swept-wing}\dictentry{Swept Wing}{A wing angled rearward from the root.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-forward-swept-wing}\dictentry{Forward Swept Wing}{A wing angled forward from the root.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-ogive}\dictentry{Ogive}{A pointed, curved shape used for nose cones.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-streamline}\dictentry{Streamline}{A line tangent to the velocity vector at every point in a flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-pathline}\dictentry{Pathline}{The trajectory traced by a fluid particle over time.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-streakline}\dictentry{Streakline}{The locus of particles passing through a fixed point.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-continuity-equation}\dictentry{Continuity Equation}{Mass conservation in fluid flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-momentum-equation}\dictentry{Momentum Equation}{Newton's second law applied to fluid motion, stating that the net force acting on a fluid element equals the rate of change of its momentum. In integral form, it relates the sum of surface and body forces to the time rate of change of momentum within a control volume plus the net flux of momentum across its boundaries. The momentum equation is fundamental in aerodynamic force analysis and propulsion system design.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-energy-equation}\dictentry{Energy Equation}{Energy conservation in fluid flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-bernoulli-equation}\dictentry{Bernoulli Equation}{Constant total pressure along a streamline in inviscid flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-camber-line}\dictentry{Camber Line}{The line midway between the upper and lower airfoil surfaces.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-mean-line}\dictentry{Mean Line}{Another term for the camber line of an airfoil.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-pressure-coefficient}\dictentry{Pressure Coefficient}{A dimensionless pressure normalized by dynamic pressure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-stagnation-point}\dictentry{Stagnation Point}{A point on a body where the flow velocity is zero.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-stagnation-pressure}\dictentry{Stagnation Pressure}{The pressure at a stagnation point.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-static-pressure}\dictentry{Static Pressure}{The ambient pressure of the flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-total-pressure}\dictentry{Total Pressure}{The sum of static and dynamic pressure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aerodynamic-twist}\dictentry{Aerodynamic Twist}{Variation of airfoil section angle along the span.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-geometric-twist}\dictentry{Geometric Twist}{Physical twist of the wing structure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wash-in}\dictentry{Wash-In}{A wing twist increasing angle of attack at the tip.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-oswald-span-efficiency}\dictentry{Oswald Span Efficiency}{A factor relating induced drag to that of an elliptical wing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-elliptical-wing}\dictentry{Elliptical Wing}{A wing with elliptical planform, giving minimum induced drag.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-taper-ratio}\dictentry{Taper Ratio}{The ratio of tip chord to root chord.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wing-loading}\dictentry{Wing Loading}{The ratio of aircraft weight to wing area.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-load-factor}\dictentry{Load Factor}{The ratio of lift to weight, measured in g.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-v-n-diagram}\dictentry{V-n Diagram}{A plot of airspeed versus load factor defining flight envelope.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-maneuverability}\dictentry{Maneuverability}{The ability of an aircraft to change direction quickly.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-agility}\dictentry{Agility}{A measure of rapid attitude change capability.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-turning-radius}\dictentry{Turning Radius}{The radius of a turn at a given speed and bank angle.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-rate-of-turn}\dictentry{Rate of Turn}{The angular velocity of an aircraft in a turn.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-coordinated-turn}\dictentry{Coordinated Turn}{A turn with no sideslip, balanced lift and centripetal force.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-sideslip}\dictentry{Sideslip}{The angle between an aircraft's longitudinal axis and the relative wind direction, indicating a lateral component of motion relative to the airflow. Sideslip affects the distribution of aerodynamic forces on the fuselage and vertical tail, and is a critical parameter in lateral-directional stability analysis, crosswind landings, and coordinated turn calculations.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-ground-effect}\dictentry{Ground Effect}{Reduced induced drag when flying close to the ground.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-reynolds-analogy}\dictentry{Reynolds Analogy}{Relation between heat transfer and skin friction.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-prandtl-number}\dictentry{Prandtl Number}{The ratio of momentum diffusivity to thermal diffusivity.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-mach-tuck}\dictentry{Mach Tuck}{A nose-down pitching moment at transonic speeds.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-shock-stall}\dictentry{Shock Stall}{Stall caused by shock-induced boundary layer separation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-buffeting}\dictentry{Buffeting}{Vibration from unsteady flow separation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-flutter}\dictentry{Flutter}{An aeroelastic oscillation that can be destructive.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-control-reversal}\dictentry{Control Reversal}{Loss or reversal of control effectiveness at high speeds.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-gust-load}\dictentry{Gust Load}{A transient load from atmospheric turbulence.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-maneuvering-load}\dictentry{Maneuvering Load}{Load from pilot-induced maneuvers.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lift-distribution}\dictentry{Lift Distribution}{Spanwise variation of lift on a wing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-trailing-vortex}\dictentry{Trailing Vortex}{The vortex rolling up from the wingtip.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-starting-vortex}\dictentry{Starting Vortex}{The vortex shed when a wing begins moving.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-horseshoe-vortex}\dictentry{Horseshoe Vortex}{The vortex system modeled as bound vortex plus trailing vortices.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-panel-method}\dictentry{Panel Method}{A numerical potential flow method using surface panels.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-vortex-lattice-method}\dictentry{Vortex Lattice Method}{A numerical method modeling surfaces with vortex rings.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-source-panel-method}\dictentry{Source Panel Method}{A panel method using source/sink distributions.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-doublet}\dictentry{Doublet}{A potential flow element combining a source and sink.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-rankine-body}\dictentry{Rankine Body}{A streamlined body shape from source-sink superposition.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-joukowski-airfoil}\dictentry{Joukowski Airfoil}{An airfoil shape generated by conformal mapping.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-thin-airfoil-theory}\dictentry{Thin Airfoil Theory}{Linearized theory predicting airfoil lift and moment.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lifting-line-theory}\dictentry{Lifting Line Theory}{Prandtl's classical aerodynamic theory for finite wings that models the wing as a bound vortex line with trailing vortices forming a vortex sheet. The theory predicts spanwise lift distribution, induced drag, and downwash, and shows that an elliptical lift distribution produces minimum induced drag. It provides the theoretical basis for understanding finite wing aerodynamics and wing design.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lifting-surface-theory}\dictentry{Lifting Surface Theory}{A more general theory for wings of arbitrary planform.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-sweep-theory}\dictentry{Sweep Theory}{Simplified method for swept wing aerodynamics.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-slender-body-theory}\dictentry{Slender Body Theory}{Theory for long, thin bodies at small angles of attack.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-area-rule}\dictentry{Area Rule}{A transonic aerodynamic design principle developed by Richard Whitcomb stating that the cross-sectional area of an aircraft should vary smoothly along its length to minimize wave drag at transonic speeds. The area rule led to the characteristic 'Coke bottle' fuselage shape of transonic aircraft and is critical for optimizing aerodynamic performance near Mach 1.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-supercritical-airfoil}\dictentry{Supercritical Airfoil}{An airfoil designed to delay drag rise at transonic speeds.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-shock-free-airfoil}\dictentry{Shock-Free Airfoil}{An airfoil designed to avoid shock waves at design conditions.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-natural-laminar-flow}\dictentry{Natural Laminar Flow}{A design approach to maintain laminar boundary layer over more of the wing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-laminar-flow-control}\dictentry{Laminar Flow Control}{Active methods to maintain laminar boundary layer.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-riblet}\dictentry{Riblet}{Microgrooves on a surface that reduce skin friction drag.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-dimple}\dictentry{Dimple}{A surface indentation used for drag reduction, as on golf balls.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-base-drag}\dictentry{Base Drag}{Drag from the low-pressure area at a blunt base.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-boattail}\dictentry{Boattail}{A tapered aft body contour to reduce base drag.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-bump}\dictentry{Bump}{A protuberance on a surface for flow control.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-fence}\dictentry{Fence}{A vertical surface on a wing to control spanwise flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wing-fence}\dictentry{Wing Fence}{A fence on the upper wing surface to prevent spanwise flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-dogtooth}\dictentry{Dogtooth}{A notch in the leading edge to generate vortices.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-leading-edge-extension}\dictentry{Leading Edge Extension}{A strake extending forward from the wing root.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lex-fence}\dictentry{LEX Fence}{A fence on the leading edge extension for vortex control.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-strakes}\dictentry{Strakes}{A sharp-edged surface for vortex generation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-chine}\dictentry{Chine}{A sharp longitudinal edge on a fuselage for lift.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-belly-flap}\dictentry{Belly Flap}{A flap on the underside for drag or lift control.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-deceleron}\dictentry{Deceleron}{A split aileron that also acts as an airbrake.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-flapperon}\dictentry{Flapperon}{A combined flap and aileron.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-taileron}\dictentry{Taileron}{A stabilator used differentially for roll control.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-stabilator}\dictentry{Stabilator}{A one-piece horizontal tail that pivots for pitch control.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-v-tail}\dictentry{V-Tail}{A tail configuration with two angled surfaces combining rudder and elevator.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-cruciform-tail}\dictentry{Cruciform Tail}{A tail with horizontal and vertical surfaces forming a cross.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-t-tail}\dictentry{T-Tail}{A tail with the horizontal surface at the top of the vertical fin.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-h-tail}\dictentry{H-Tail}{A twin vertical tail configuration.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-twin-tail}\dictentry{Twin Tail}{Two separate vertical fins.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-endplate}\dictentry{Endplate}{A flat plate at the wingtip to reduce induced drag.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-hoerner-tip}\dictentry{Hoerner Tip}{A rounded, drooped wingtip design for drag reduction.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-raked-wingtip}\dictentry{Raked Wingtip}{A wingtip with a swept trailing edge.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-tip-fence}\dictentry{Tip Fence}{A small vertical surface at the wingtip.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wingtip-vortex}\dictentry{Wingtip Vortex}{The spiral vortex shed from a wingtip.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-contrail}\dictentry{Contrail}{A condensation trail from aircraft engine exhaust.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-vapor-cone}\dictentry{Vapor Cone}{A visible cone of condensed water vapor around an aircraft at transonic speeds.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-shock-diamond}\dictentry{Shock Diamond}{Visible diamond-shaped patterns in supersonic exhaust plumes.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-mach-wave}\dictentry{Mach Wave}{A weak wave at the Mach angle in supersonic flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-bow-shock}\dictentry{Bow Shock}{A detached shock wave ahead of a blunt body.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-attached-shock}\dictentry{Attached Shock}{A shock attached to a sharp leading edge.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lambda-shock}\dictentry{Lambda Shock}{A lambda-shaped shock in transonic flow over airfoils.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-fish-tail-shock}\dictentry{Fish-Tail Shock}{A shock pattern resembling a fish tail.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-detached-shock}\dictentry{Detached Shock}{A shock wave not in contact with the body.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-expansion-fan}\dictentry{Expansion Fan}{A fan of expansion waves around a convex corner.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-prandtl-meyer-function}\dictentry{Prandtl-Meyer Function}{The angle function for Prandtl-Meyer expansion.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-method-of-characteristics}\dictentry{Method of Characteristics}{A mathematical method for solving supersonic flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-supersonic-wind-tunnel}\dictentry{Supersonic Wind Tunnel}{A wind tunnel designed for Mach numbers above one.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-hypersonic-wind-tunnel}\dictentry{Hypersonic Wind Tunnel}{A wind tunnel for Mach numbers above five.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-shock-tunnel}\dictentry{Shock Tunnel}{A tunnel using shock waves to produce high-enthalpy flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-arc-heated-wind-tunnel}\dictentry{Arc-Heated Wind Tunnel}{A tunnel using an electric arc to heat the test gas.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-cryogenic-wind-tunnel}\dictentry{Cryogenic Wind Tunnel}{A tunnel using cold nitrogen gas to increase Reynolds number.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-indraft-wind-tunnel}\dictentry{Indraft Wind Tunnel}{A tunnel drawing air from the room through the test section.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-open-circuit-tunnel}\dictentry{Open Circuit Tunnel}{A wind tunnel with no return circuit.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-closed-circuit-tunnel}\dictentry{Closed Circuit Tunnel}{A wind tunnel with a continuous return circuit.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-low-speed-tunnel}\dictentry{Low Speed Tunnel}{A wind tunnel for Mach numbers below 0.3.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-high-speed-tunnel}\dictentry{High Speed Tunnel}{A wind tunnel for transonic and supersonic testing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wall-interference}\dictentry{Wall Interference}{The effect of wind tunnel walls on measured results.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-blockage}\dictentry{Blockage}{The ratio of model frontal area to tunnel cross-section.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-turbulence-intensity}\dictentry{Turbulence Intensity}{A measure of the fluctuation level in wind tunnel flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-flow-angularity}\dictentry{Flow Angularity}{The deviation of flow direction from the tunnel axis.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-sting-mount}\dictentry{Sting Mount}{A support strut holding a model from behind.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-strut-mount}\dictentry{Strut Mount}{A support structure holding a model from below or above.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-magnetic-suspension}\dictentry{Magnetic Suspension}{A model support system using magnetic fields.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-pressure-sensitive-paint}\dictentry{Pressure Sensitive Paint}{A paint that fluoresces in proportion to surface pressure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-temperature-sensitive-paint}\dictentry{Temperature Sensitive Paint}{A paint indicating surface temperature.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-infrared-thermography}\dictentry{Infrared Thermography}{An imaging technique for surface temperature measurement.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-optical-strain-measurement}\dictentry{Optical Strain Measurement}{A non-contact technique using cameras to measure deformation.}{catAD}
\glsadd{D-accelerometer}\dictentry{Accelerometer}{A sensor measuring acceleration for vibration testing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-strain-gauge}\dictentry{Strain Gauge}{A sensor measuring strain on a surface.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-thermocouple}\dictentry{Thermocouple}{A temperature sensor used in aerodynamic testing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{SS-balance}\dictentry{Balance}{A multi-component force measurement device.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-external-balance}\dictentry{External Balance}{A balance mounted outside the wind tunnel.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AE-internal-balance}\dictentry{Internal Balance}{A balance mounted inside the model.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-half-model-testing}\dictentry{Half Model Testing}{Testing a half-span model against a wall.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-full-model-testing}\dictentry{Full Model Testing}{Testing a complete model.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-dynamic-testing}\dictentry{Dynamic Testing}{Testing with time-varying conditions.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-static-testing}\dictentry{Static Testing}{Testing under steady conditions.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-oscillatory-testing}\dictentry{Oscillatory Testing}{Testing with oscillating model or flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-grid-turbulence}\dictentry{Grid Turbulence}{Turbulence generated by a grid in the tunnel.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-hot-wire-anemometry}\dictentry{Hot Wire Anemometry}{A measurement technique using heated wires for velocity.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-laser-doppler-velocimetry}\dictentry{Laser Doppler Velocimetry}{An optical velocity measurement technique.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-phase-doppler-anemometry}\dictentry{Phase Doppler Anemometry}{A technique measuring particle size and velocity.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-pressure-scanner}\dictentry{Pressure Scanner}{A multi-port pressure measurement system.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-wind-tunnel-fan}\dictentry{Wind Tunnel Fan}{The fan driving airflow in a wind tunnel.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-honeycomb-straightener}\dictentry{Honeycomb Straightener}{A flow conditioning device consisting of a bundle of small-diameter tubes or cells placed upstream of the contraction cone in a wind tunnel. It reduces turbulence and swirl by breaking up large-scale eddies and aligning the flow with the tunnel axis, improving flow quality in the test section.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-settling-chamber}\dictentry{Settling Chamber}{The wide-section region of a wind tunnel located between the flow conditioning screens and the contraction cone. It provides a low-velocity environment where turbulence decays and flow uniformity is established before acceleration into the test section.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-contraction-cone}\dictentry{Contraction Cone}{The converging section of a wind tunnel that accelerates the flow from the settling chamber to the test section. The contraction ratio (area ratio from inlet to outlet) determines the velocity increase and flow uniformity, with higher ratios producing lower turbulence levels.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-diffuser-section}\dictentry{Diffuser Section}{The expanding section of a wind tunnel located downstream of the test section, designed to decelerate the flow and recover static pressure. A well-designed diffuser minimizes pressure losses and improves overall tunnel efficiency.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-corner-vanes}\dictentry{Corner Vanes}{Curved guide vanes installed in the corners of closed-circuit wind tunnels to turn the flow with minimal pressure loss and flow separation. They are essential for maintaining flow quality and reducing the power required to drive the tunnel.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-turning-vanes}\dictentry{Turning Vanes}{Aerodynamic surfaces placed in duct bends or wind tunnel corners to redirect airflow efficiently. They prevent flow separation and reduce total pressure losses by guiding the flow around turns.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-screens}\dictentry{Screens}{Fine-mesh grids placed in the settling chamber of a wind tunnel to reduce turbulence intensity and improve flow uniformity. Multiple screens with decreasing mesh size are often used in series to achieve high-quality flow.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-acoustic-testing}\dictentry{Acoustic Testing}{A specialized wind tunnel testing methodology focused on measuring noise generated by aerodynamic flows. It requires anechoic or treated test sections with low background noise levels to accurately capture airframe, engine, and propeller noise signatures.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aeroacoustics}\dictentry{Aeroacoustics}{The branch of acoustics that studies noise generation by turbulent fluid motion or aerodynamic forces interacting with surfaces. It encompasses jet noise, airframe noise, propeller and rotor noise, and fan noise, and is critical for aircraft certification and community noise reduction.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-jet-noise}\dictentry{Jet Noise}{The noise generated by the high-velocity exhaust of a jet engine, resulting from turbulent mixing of the jet plume with the ambient air. Jet noise is a major component of aircraft noise during takeoff and is reduced through chevron nozzles and serrated ejectors.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-airframe-noise}\dictentry{Airframe Noise}{The aerodynamic noise produced by the airframe of an aircraft during flight, including noise from landing gear, flaps, slats, and wing trailing edges. As engines become quieter, airframe noise has become a dominant contributor to overall aircraft noise during approach and landing.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-propeller-noise}\dictentry{Propeller Noise}{The noise generated by rotating propellers due to blade loading, thickness effects, and tip vortices. It consists of tonal components at the blade passage frequency and broadband noise from turbulent boundary layer interactions.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-rotor-noise}\dictentry{Rotor Noise}{The acoustic signature of helicopter main rotors and tail rotors, including blade-vortex interaction noise, high-speed impulsive noise, and broadband turbulence ingestion noise. Rotor noise is a critical consideration for urban air mobility and military operations.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-fan-noise}\dictentry{Fan Noise}{The noise produced by the fan stage of turbofan engines, comprising tonal noise at the blade passage frequency and broadband noise from turbulent interactions. Fan noise is a significant contributor to aircraft noise during takeoff and approach.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-combustion-noise}\dictentry{Combustion Noise}{The noise generated by unsteady heat release and turbulent mixing in the combustion chamber of a gas turbine engine. Combustion noise contributes to the overall engine noise signature and is influenced by fuel-air mixing and combustor geometry.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-noise-certification}\dictentry{Noise Certification}{The regulatory process by which aircraft are certified to meet maximum allowable noise levels established by aviation authorities such as ICAO, FAA, and EASA. Noise certification defines measurement procedures, flight test conditions, and noise limits for takeoff, approach, and sideline.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-noise-reduction}\dictentry{Noise Reduction}{Techniques and technologies employed to reduce aircraft noise, including engine nacelle treatments, chevron nozzles, acoustic liners, shielding by airframe structures, and operational procedures such as continuous descent approaches.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-chevron-nozzle}\dictentry{Chevron Nozzle}{A jet engine exhaust nozzle with a serrated or sawtooth trailing edge that promotes mixing between the hot exhaust jet and the ambient air. Chevrons reduce jet noise by breaking up large-scale turbulent structures and shifting the noise spectrum to higher frequencies.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-hush-kit}\dictentry{Hush Kit}{A set of modifications applied to an aircraft engine or nacelle to reduce noise emissions, typically consisting of acoustic liners, exhaust mixers, and nozzle treatments. Hush kits allow older aircraft to meet modern noise certification standards.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-emission-index}\dictentry{Emission Index}{A quantitative measure of pollutant mass produced per unit mass of fuel burned in an aircraft engine, expressed in grams of pollutant per kilogram of fuel. Emission indices for NOx, CO, unburned hydrocarbons, and soot are used to assess engine environmental performance.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-carbon-footprint}\dictentry{Carbon Footprint}{The total amount of carbon dioxide (CO2) emissions attributable to an aircraft or aviation operation over a given period or mission. Aviation carbon footprint reduction strategies include sustainable aviation fuels, improved aerodynamics, lightweight structures, and optimized flight operations.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AE-sustainable-aviation-fuel}\dictentry{Sustainable Aviation Fuel}{A liquid fuel derived from renewable or waste feedstocks such as used cooking oil, agricultural residues, or municipal waste, designed to be a drop-in replacement for conventional jet fuel. Sustainable aviation fuel can reduce lifecycle CO2 emissions by up to 80\% compared to fossil-based jet fuel.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-hydrogen-propulsion}\dictentry{Hydrogen Propulsion}{The use of hydrogen as a fuel for aircraft propulsion, either burned in modified gas turbine engines or used in fuel cells to power electric motors. Hydrogen offers zero CO2 emissions at the point of use but presents challenges in storage volume, cryogenic handling, and infrastructure.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-electric-propulsion}\dictentry{Electric Propulsion}{Aircraft propulsion systems that use electric motors powered by batteries, fuel cells, or hybrid generators to drive propellers or fans. Electric propulsion enables distributed propulsion configurations, reduces local emissions, and opens new vehicle architectures for urban air mobility.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-hybrid-propulsion}\dictentry{Hybrid Propulsion}{A propulsion architecture combining a conventional thermal engine (turbine or piston) with an electric motor and battery system. Hybrid propulsion allows the thermal engine to operate at optimum efficiency while the electric motor provides peak power for takeoff and climb.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-solar-powered-flight}\dictentry{Solar Powered Flight}{Aircraft propulsion using photovoltaic cells mounted on the wing and fuselage surfaces to convert sunlight into electrical energy for motors and batteries. Solar powered aircraft can achieve sustained flight at high altitudes for extended durations, limited by energy storage and diurnal cycles.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-high-altitude-flight}\dictentry{High Altitude Flight}{Flight operations at altitudes above 50,000 feet, where low air density requires high aerodynamic efficiency and specialized propulsion systems. High altitude flight includes stratospheric platforms for communications, surveillance, and atmospheric science.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-stratospheric-flight}\dictentry{Stratospheric Flight}{Flight within the stratosphere (approximately 35,000 to 160,000 feet), characterized by stable atmospheric conditions, low turbulence, and minimal weather. Stratospheric platforms enable long-endurance missions for earth observation, telecommunications, and scientific research.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-suborbital-flight}\dictentry{Suborbital Flight}{A trajectory that reaches space (above the Karman line at 100 km altitude) but lacks the velocity to achieve orbit, resulting in a ballistic return to Earth. Suborbital flight is used for sounding rockets, crewed space tourism, and hypersonic research.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-atmospheric-re-entry}\dictentry{Atmospheric Re-entry}{The phase of a spacecraft's return trajectory from space through a planetary atmosphere, characterized by hypersonic velocities, intense aerodynamic heating, and high deceleration loads. Atmospheric re-entry requires thermal protection systems and careful trajectory design to ensure survival.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-re-entry-corridor}\dictentry{Re-entry Corridor}{The allowable range of flight path angles within which a re-entering spacecraft must fly to achieve a safe landing at the intended site. Deviations outside the corridor result in excessive heating, structural failure, or landing far from the target.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-re-entry-angle}\dictentry{Re-entry Angle}{The angle between the flight path of a re-entering vehicle and the local horizontal at the point of atmospheric entry. A steep re-entry angle increases peak heating rates and deceleration, while a shallow angle extends the heating duration and may cause skip-out.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-skip-re-entry}\dictentry{Skip Re-entry}{A re-entry trajectory in which the vehicle descends into the upper atmosphere, generates aerodynamic lift to ascend back out of the atmosphere, then re-enters a second time. Skip re-entry extends the range and allows higher entry velocities while managing peak heating.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-ballistic-re-entry}\dictentry{Ballistic Re-entry}{A non-lifting re-entry trajectory in which the vehicle follows a purely gravitational path with no aerodynamic lift. Ballistic re-entry produces high peak deceleration and heating rates but is simpler and was used by early crewed capsules such as Mercury and Apollo.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-lifting-re-entry}\dictentry{Lifting Re-entry}{A re-entry trajectory that uses aerodynamic lift generated by the vehicle body or wings to control the descent path and reduce peak deceleration. Lifting re-entry, used by the Space Shuttle and proposed for crewed Mars missions, enables cross-range maneuvering and gentler deceleration.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-re-entry-velocity}\dictentry{Re-entry Velocity}{The speed at which a spacecraft enters a planetary atmosphere, typically between 7.8 km/s (low Earth orbit) and 11.2 km/s (escape velocity from Earth). The re-entry velocity determines the kinetic energy that must be dissipated as heat during atmospheric passage.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-re-entry-heating-rate}\dictentry{Re-entry Heating Rate}{The rate at which thermal energy is transferred to a spacecraft surface during atmospheric re-entry, typically expressed in watts per square centimeter (W/cm2). The peak heating rate occurs at the point of maximum deceleration and drives thermal protection system design.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-peak-heating}\dictentry{Peak Heating}{The maximum instantaneous heating rate experienced by a spacecraft during atmospheric re-entry, occurring when the combination of velocity and atmospheric density produces the highest convective and radiative heat flux. Peak heating determines the required thickness and material of the thermal protection system.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-heat-flux}\dictentry{Heat Flux}{The rate of thermal energy transfer per unit area, expressed in watts per square meter (W/m2), at a surface exposed to aerodynamic heating. Heat flux is a critical parameter in the design of thermal protection systems and is composed of convective, radiative, and catalytic components.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-convective-heating}\dictentry{Convective Heating}{The component of aerodynamic heating resulting from the transfer of thermal energy from the hot boundary layer gas to the vehicle surface through conduction and convection. Convective heating dominates at moderate re-entry velocities and is influenced by boundary layer state (laminar or turbulent).}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-radiative-heating}\dictentry{Radiative Heating}{The component of aerodynamic heating resulting from thermal radiation emitted by the high-temperature shock layer gas, particularly at hypersonic velocities above 10 km/s. Radiative heating becomes dominant at very high re-entry velocities and is a major consideration for Mars return and planetary entry.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-catalytic-heating}\dictentry{Catalytic Heating}{The additional heating that occurs when atomic species (oxygen and nitrogen) recombine on a spacecraft surface during re-entry, releasing chemical energy. Catalytic heating depends on the surface material's recombination efficiency and can significantly increase total heat flux.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-thermal-protection-material}\dictentry{Thermal Protection Material}{Materials specifically engineered to withstand the extreme heat loads of atmospheric re-entry or hypersonic flight. Thermal protection materials include ablative composites (carbon-phenolic), reusable ceramics (tiles), and advanced carbon-carbon composites, each selected for specific temperature ranges and mission profiles.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-ablative-tps}\dictentry{Ablative TPS}{A thermal protection system that dissipates heat through the controlled ablation (surface removal) of a sacrificial material, carrying heat away from the underlying structure. Ablative TPS is used for high-heat-flux applications such as ICBM re-entry vehicles and planetary probes.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-reusable-tps}\dictentry{Reusable TPS}{A thermal protection system designed to withstand multiple re-entry cycles without replacement, typically using ceramic tiles, fibrous insulation blankets, or metallic panels. Reusable TPS was developed for the Space Shuttle and is essential for affordable reusable launch vehicles.}{catAD}
\glsadd{RM-insulation}\dictentry{Insulation}{Materials with low thermal conductivity placed between the hot outer surface and the underlying structure to reduce heat transfer. In aerospace applications, fibrous insulation blankets, aerogels, and multilayer insulation are used to protect sensitive components from high temperatures.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-thermal-barrier-coating}\dictentry{Thermal Barrier Coating}{A thin layer of ceramic material applied to metallic components to reduce heat transfer and protect against oxidation and thermal fatigue. Thermal barrier coatings are used on turbine blades, combustion chambers, and exhaust nozzles in gas turbine engines.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-hot-structure}\dictentry{Hot Structure}{An aerospace structural design philosophy in which the primary load-bearing structure is allowed to reach high temperatures during flight, eliminating the need for heavy insulation. Hot structures are typically made of high-temperature alloys, titanium, or ceramic matrix composites.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-cold-structure}\dictentry{Cold Structure}{An aerospace structural design approach in which the primary load-bearing structure is protected from high temperatures by external insulation, maintaining it at relatively low temperatures. Cold structures allow the use of conventional materials like aluminum but add insulation weight.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-nose-cap}\dictentry{Nose Cap}{The forward-most component of a re-entry vehicle or hypersonic aircraft, exposed to the highest stagnation temperatures and pressures. The nose cap is typically made of reinforced carbon-carbon or an ablative material and must withstand extreme thermal and mechanical loads.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-leading-edge-tps}\dictentry{Leading Edge TPS}{The thermal protection system applied to the leading edges of wings, fins, and control surfaces of re-entry vehicles and hypersonic aircraft. Leading edge TPS must withstand high heat fluxes while maintaining aerodynamic shape and structural integrity.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-tiles}\dictentry{Tiles}{Rigid ceramic insulating blocks used as reusable thermal protection on the Space Shuttle and other re-entry vehicles. Tiles are made of high-purity silica fibers and are coated with a borosilicate glass coating for waterproofing and emissivity control.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-reinforced-carbon-carbon}\dictentry{Reinforced Carbon Carbon}{A composite material consisting of carbon fibers embedded in a carbon matrix, capable of withstanding temperatures up to 3000F (1650C). Reinforced carbon-carbon is used for the highest-temperature areas of re-entry vehicles, including nose caps and wing leading edges.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-flexible-tps}\dictentry{Flexible TPS}{A lightweight, conformable thermal protection material made of ceramic or polymer fibers, used for areas with moderate heating on re-entry vehicles. Flexible TPS blankets can be draped over curved surfaces and are easier to install than rigid tiles.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-inflatable-tps}\dictentry{Inflatable TPS}{A deployable thermal protection system consisting of a flexible, inflatable aeroshell that decelerates a payload during atmospheric entry. Inflatable TPS enables larger drag areas than rigid aeroshells of the same mass, reducing peak heating and deceleration.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-aeroshell}\dictentry{Aeroshell}{The protective outer shell of a spacecraft that provides aerodynamic deceleration and thermal protection during atmospheric entry. An aeroshell typically consists of a heat shield on the forward face and a backshell covering the aft side, enclosing the payload.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AE-heat-shield}\dictentry{Heat Shield}{The forward-facing component of an aeroshell that absorbs and dissipates the intense heat generated during atmospheric entry. Heat shields may be ablative (consumed during entry) or reusable (ceramic tiles) depending on the mission requirements.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-buoyancy}\dictentry{Buoyancy}{The upward force exerted by a fluid on a body immersed in it, equal to the weight of the fluid displaced by the body (Archimedes' principle). In aerodynamics, buoyancy is the principle behind lighter-than-air vehicles such as balloons and airships, and is proportional to the density difference between the lifting gas and the surrounding air.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-clean-configuration}\dictentry{Clean Configuration}{The flight configuration of an aircraft in which all high-lift devices (flaps, slats) and landing gear are fully retracted, minimizing drag and maximizing airspeed for a given power setting. Clean configuration is used for cruise, climb, and high-speed flight.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-decalage}\dictentry{Decalage}{The angular difference between the chord lines of the upper and lower wings of a biplane, measured in degrees. Positive decalage (upper wing at a higher angle of incidence) results in greater lift from the upper wing and is used to tailor stall characteristics and longitudinal stability.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-disk-loading}\dictentry{Disk Loading}{The average pressure change across an actuator disk such as a propeller or rotor, defined as the thrust divided by the disk area. Low disk loading is characteristic of rotors and high-lift propellers, while high disk loading is typical of high-speed propellers and ducted fans.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-enstrophy}\dictentry{Enstrophy}{A quantity in fluid dynamics that measures the integrated square of the vorticity magnitude, representing the dissipation of kinetic energy in turbulent flows. Enstrophy is particularly useful in the study of turbulence and is directly related to the turbulent kinetic energy dissipation rate.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-keel-effect}\dictentry{Keel Effect}{The aerodynamic phenomenon where lateral area located behind the center of gravity provides directional stability, analogous to the keel of a ship. In aircraft design, the keel effect is provided by the vertical tail and aft fuselage and contributes to weathercock stability.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-rogallo-wing}\dictentry{Rogallo Wing}{A flexible, delta-shaped wing design consisting of two conically shaped fabric surfaces held taut by a rigid frame or inflated tubes. Rogallo wings are used in hang gliders, parawings, and some spacecraft recovery systems for their light weight and deployability.}{catAD}
\glsadd{AD-viscosity}\dictentry{Viscosity}{A measure of a fluid's resistance to shear deformation, arising from intermolecular forces and momentum transfer between fluid layers. In aerodynamics, viscosity governs the behavior of the boundary layer, determines skin friction drag, and influences flow separation, transition to turbulence, and heat transfer.}{catAD}
\end{multicols}

\clearpage
\chapter*{Aerospace}
\addcontentsline{toc}{chapter}{Aerospace}
\markboth{Aerospace}{Aerospace}
\clearpage
\begin{multicols}{2}
\small
Aircraft and spacecraft systems: avionics, propulsion (non-rocket), flight controls, airframe structures, and the engineered systems that enable flight.\par
\vspace{0.5cm}
\glsadd{AE-aerospace-engineering}\dictentry{Aerospace Engineering}{The primary field covering aircraft and spacecraft development.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-aircraft}\dictentry{Aircraft}{Any vehicle designed to travel through the air.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-airplane}\dictentry{Airplane}{A powered fixed-wing aircraft.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-gyrocopter}\dictentry{Gyrocopter}{A rotorcraft with an unpowered rotor in autorotation.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-glider}\dictentry{Glider}{A fixed-wing aircraft without an engine.}{catAE}
\glsadd{D-uav}\dictentry{UAV}{An unmanned aerial vehicle flown without onboard pilot.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-evtol}\dictentry{eVTOL}{An electric vertical takeoff and landing aircraft.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-balloon}\dictentry{Balloon}{An unpowered lighter-than-air craft.}{catAE}
\glsadd{SS-spacecraft}\dictentry{Spacecraft}{A vehicle operating beyond Earth's atmosphere.}{catAE}
\glsadd{SS-satellite}\dictentry{Satellite}{An artificial object in orbit around a celestial body.}{catAE}
\glsadd{RM-launch-vehicle}\dictentry{Launch Vehicle}{A rocket propelling payload from Earth into orbit.}{catAE}
\glsadd{RM-sounding-rocket}\dictentry{Sounding Rocket}{A suborbital rocket for upper atmosphere experiments.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-spaceplane}\dictentry{Spaceplane}{A vehicle operating in both atmosphere and space.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-hypersonic-vehicle}\dictentry{Hypersonic Vehicle}{A craft capable of sustained flight above Mach 5.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-flight-mechanics}\dictentry{Flight Mechanics}{The study of forces and motion of aircraft in flight.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-sideslip-angle}\dictentry{Sideslip Angle}{The angle between the aircraft axis and relative wind.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-flight-path}\dictentry{Flight Path}{The trajectory of the aircraft's center of gravity.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-glide-ratio}\dictentry{Glide Ratio}{Horizontal distance per altitude lost, equal to L/D.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-ceiling}\dictentry{Ceiling}{The maximum altitude an aircraft can reach.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-stall-recovery}\dictentry{Stall Recovery}{Techniques to regain control from a stalled condition.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-spin-recovery}\dictentry{Spin Recovery}{Techniques to recover from an autorotative spin.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-downwash}\dictentry{Downwash}{Air deflected downward by a wing in producing lift.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-flow-separation}\dictentry{Flow Separation}{Boundary layer detachment causing lift loss.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-fuselage}\dictentry{Fuselage}{The central body of an aircraft.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-wing-spar}\dictentry{Wing Spar}{The principal spanwise structural member of a wing.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-rib}\dictentry{Rib}{A chordwise airfoil-shaped structural member of a wing.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-stringer}\dictentry{Stringer}{A thin longitudinal structural reinforcing element.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-bulkhead}\dictentry{Bulkhead}{A transverse partition carrying concentrated loads.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-longeron}\dictentry{Longeron}{A major longitudinal fuselage member.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-empennage}\dictentry{Empennage}{The tail assembly providing stability and control.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-vertical-stabilizer}\dictentry{Vertical Stabilizer}{The fixed vertical tail surface for directional stability.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-horizontal-stabilizer}\dictentry{Horizontal Stabilizer}{The fixed horizontal tail surface for longitudinal stability.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-fin}\dictentry{Fin}{Another term for the vertical stabilizer.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-skin}\dictentry{Skin}{The outer covering transferring aerodynamic loads.}{catAE}
\glsadd{SS-honeycomb-panel}\dictentry{Honeycomb Panel}{A lightweight composite panel with a honeycomb core.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-monocoque}\dictentry{Monocoque}{A structure where the outer skin carries main stresses.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-semi-monocoque}\dictentry{Semi-Monocoque}{A stressed skin structure with stringer and bulkhead reinforcement.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-aluminum-alloy}\dictentry{Aluminum Alloy}{Lightweight alloy extensively used in aircraft.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-titanium-alloy}\dictentry{Titanium Alloy}{High-strength corrosion-resistant metal for high-temperature parts.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-carbon-fiber}\dictentry{Carbon Fiber}{A strong, stiff, lightweight composite material.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-fiberglass}\dictentry{Fiberglass}{Glass fibers embedded in polymer matrix.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-kevlar}\dictentry{Kevlar}{A strong heat-resistant synthetic fiber.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-composite-material}\dictentry{Composite Material}{A material combining two or more constituent materials.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-superalloy}\dictentry{Superalloy}{An alloy with high strength and creep resistance at high temperatures.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-shape-memory-alloy}\dictentry{Shape Memory Alloy}{An alloy returning to shape when heated after deformation.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-ablative-material}\dictentry{Ablative Material}{A material eroding to carry away heat.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-electrical-system}\dictentry{Electrical System}{The system providing electrical power to the aircraft.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-fuel-system}\dictentry{Fuel System}{The system storing and delivering fuel to engines.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-environmental-control-system}\dictentry{Environmental Control System}{The system managing cabin pressure, temperature, and ventilation.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-cabin-pressurization}\dictentry{Cabin Pressurization}{Maintaining safe cabin altitude at high flight levels.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-landing-gear}\dictentry{Landing Gear}{The undercarriage supporting the aircraft on the ground.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-braking-system}\dictentry{Braking System}{Components decelerating the aircraft on the runway.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-anti-skid-system}\dictentry{Anti-Skid System}{A safety system preventing wheel lock during braking.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-ice-protection-system}\dictentry{Ice Protection System}{A system preventing ice on wings and tail.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-gps}\dictentry{GPS}{Satellite-based global positioning system.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-gnss}\dictentry{GNSS}{Global Navigation Satellite Systems: GPS, GLONASS, Galileo, BeiDou.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-dead-reckoning}\dictentry{Dead Reckoning}{Position estimation from speed, time, and direction.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-waypoint}\dictentry{Waypoint}{A predetermined set of coordinates on a flight route.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-great-circle-route}\dictentry{Great Circle Route}{The shortest path between two points on a sphere.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-magnetic-heading}\dictentry{Magnetic Heading}{Aircraft direction relative to magnetic north.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-true-heading}\dictentry{True Heading}{Aircraft direction relative to true north.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-indicated-airspeed}\dictentry{Indicated Airspeed}{Speed read directly from the airspeed indicator.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-true-airspeed}\dictentry{True Airspeed}{Airspeed relative to undisturbed air.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-ground-speed}\dictentry{Ground Speed}{Speed relative to the ground.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-turboprop}\dictentry{Turboprop}{A jet engine driving a propeller.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-pulsejet}\dictentry{Pulsejet}{A jet engine operating by intermittent combustion.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-afterburner}\dictentry{Afterburner}{A secondary combustion chamber for thrust augmentation.}{catAE}
\glsadd{RM-specific-impulse}\dictentry{Specific Impulse}{A measure of propellant efficiency in seconds.}{catAE}
\glsadd{RM-thrust-vector-control}\dictentry{Thrust Vector Control}{Redirecting engine thrust for vehicle control.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-hot-staging}\dictentry{Hot Staging}{Upper stage ignition before lower stage separation.}{catAE}

\glsadd{SS-sun-sensor}\dictentry{Sun Sensor}{A sensor detecting the Sun direction.}{catAE}

\glsadd{SS-airlock}\dictentry{Airlock}{A chamber maintaining pressure integrity during passage.}{catAE}

\glsadd{AE-hohmann-transfer}\dictentry{Hohmann Transfer}{An efficient elliptical transfer between circular orbits.}{catAE}
\glsadd{AE-orbital-inclination}\dictentry{Orbital Inclination}{The angle between the orbital plane and the reference plane.}{catAE}

\glsadd{SS-orbital-period}\dictentry{Orbital Period}{Time for one complete revolution.}{catAE}
